the child before she thought about the necklace. A small boy was trapped in an old service lift. He had climbed inside during hide-and-seek, and the door had shut. Cassian caught the next approaching tray before it struck him. The chef cleared the passage, abandoning dessert with no visible grief.","The executive director called the maintenance team. The chief executive took orders from the teenage instructor, who knew the safest path around the stored equipment. A champion swimmer and his coach helped move guests out of the way. The cheerleader kept the younger children with her. Nobody made the boy a spectacle or another casualty of adult panic.","The lift's failure could cripple a child's trust in unfamiliar places. Cassian would see the boy as a frightened guest, not an inconvenience. An emissary from the hostess carried a blanket, while the classicist fetched water. The kitchen might be twenty-five degrees Celsius, but the old shaft felt cold. Their contemplative clergyman put his coat beside the opening.","The hostess asked the guy in the dragon mask to stay in the quiet room with his infant niece. Granny joined them. A grandparent had a useful capacity for waiting without making a child feel abandoned. The Catholic elder called for a clergyman, then remembered the nearest one was holding a mop. He went to help instead.","Mara could perceive someone slipping along the terrace. The silver mask disappeared beneath a curtain. Her ruby might be there. A moment later, a bilingual Briton pointed to a dropped fastening. He had seen the woman trade a small bag with a waiter. The waiter denied being involved. A freelance correspondent confirmed he had been carrying dirty dishes throughout the blackout.","The retired diplomat asked a local detective to assist. He was attending as a diner, not to oppress a suspect with an amateur interrogation. He asked guests to stay voluntarily while staff checked doors. The hotel would not misrepresent the request as an official detention. An immigrant baker with limited local language asked for a translation; the bilingual guest provided it calmly.","The headhunter went in search of the engineer, leaving the recipe contributor to guide him. A local inhabitant knew which door opened to the service yard. Her undivided attention got them there quickly. The idealist benefactor followed with a torch. His hopeful promise to help had finally acquired a useful shape.","A benefactor suggested offering the thief a reward. The cynic called that a ransom. A Democrat said the words mattered less than locating the missing piece. The economist reminded them that an incentive might encourage a false claim. The controversialist wanted to debate all three positions. The cashier asked him to hold a torch. He found it unexpectedly difficult to argue while useful.","'Darling, stop trying to divine a solution from the ceiling,' Granny told her husband. Domestic familiarity cut through his alarm. He admitted, with frank embarrassment, that he had become a helpless captive to imagined disasters. Mara had felt something comparable. None of them was immune to fear, regardless of experience.","The geneticist and the generalist had gone to help the maintenance team read a faded diagram. Neither claimed to be an engineer. The geographer traced the passage route instead. The federal museum visitor held a lamp. The historian found an old plan behind the framed annal in the lobby. A manuscript expert could sometimes be handy in a mechanical emergency after all.","Their engineering competitor had been the hired repair firm, whose service was supposedly inferior to nobody's. Its boast did not evaluate this particular fault. The staff needed to regulate access to the old lift now. Everyone could get to a quieter space while the team worked. A person worn out by noise could ask to leave without explaining themselves.","Cassian spoke to the boy through the panel. The child's breath had steadied. The hotel's retired headmaster told a story while the team worked. Their efforts were systematic, using power sparingly until an engineer isolated the failed circuit. The instructor's developmental training had included emergency roles; she now gave her students a chance to observe rather than improvise.","A developer offered tools from his car. The environmentalist fetched them with the explorer, who incidentally still carried his fossil. A gentleman held every door on the way. The kitchen's waste plan had to wait; its people mattered more than processed labels. The chef kept the room operational while giving up the glamorous menu. Nobody was worse off for eating sandwiches.","Mara found the silver mask in a cupboard. Beneath it lay a small stolen bag. The ruby necklace was inside, together with the missing recipe card. The thief had apparently taken anything that looked valuable. A prestigious jewel and an elder's dinner instructions had become comparable trophies.","The little boy's voice returned, clear and angry. Cassian felt a titanic surge of relief. He asked the nearest people to unite their efforts on moving the barrier, leaving the engineer room to work.","The little bag could be used to smuggle jewels, but the recipe card made its contents seem absurd. The thief had perhaps hoped to misrepresent it as ordinary shopping. Mara left it with the detective. He handled the situation beautifully, giving no guest a free opportunity to become a heroic amateur prosecutor.","The maintenance engineer opened the lift. The boy emerged into Cassian's arms, then demanded his mother. Mara felt her own knees weaken. Cassian met her eyes over the child's head. The necklace suddenly looked like a very small thing."],"questions":[],"wordCount":924,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0230\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div His first story made the lift sound like a titanic cave full of dragons. His second admitted he had hidden there to avoid dancing. The instructor listened without humiliating him. Next time he could refuse a dance in words, she said. He did not need a mechanical escape route.","The detective recovered the missing items. The person who had worn the silver mask had left by a service door and been stopped by a security guard. The hotel would cooperate with the inquiry, and the witnesses would give accounts of what they actually saw. The story did not require an immediate public confession to feel complete. Mara was content to let the officer do his work.","The chef returned the recipe card to the retired headmaster. He promised to try the old dish on a quieter evening, after checking the quantities. The card had survived two accidents now. Its owner asked him to use it rather than frame it. Food was a way to unite people, not another reason to make them watch.","Cassian came back to Mara with the necklace in a little cloth. “May I?”","She turned. His fingers brushed the nape of her neck as he fastened it. The familiar contact carried more reassurance than the ruby's return. He kissed the place beneath the clasp. She caught his hand before he could step away.","“I wasn't worried about the stone,” she said.","“I know.”","He did not add that his own fear had been less well disciplined. The child behind a wall, the blackout, Mara suddenly out of sight: his mind had supplied too many possibilities. She could perceive them in the way his hand remained warm and firm around hers. She pulled him towards the terrace, where there was room to breathe.","The hotel would review the lift's use. The old mechanism had been disconnected from guest access but a temporary latch had failed. A child should not have had the chance to reach it. The accident might have been worse, and nobody intended to pretend otherwise. It was a practical problem to repair, regardless of the recovered jewels.","The executive director wanted to oppress the evening's memory with an apology speech. The hostess stopped her. She could thank people, explain the closure and let them eat. The team had used its emergency roles well. The sandwiches had been good. Those two things could be true without making the failure acceptable.","The tables moved closer together. The musician tried a quieter tune. A comic asked whether he should tell jokes. The mother of the rescued boy said only if they were good. He considered that a more demanding instruction than usual. The room laughed, and the sound was joyful rather than dutiful.","Processed ingredients had fed them perfectly well. The systematic waste plan would resume tomorrow. The chef was not worse off for surrendering an ornate dessert, and the guests could see the dinner as a success. They had not lost their ability to unite simply because a planned performance had failed.","The clock enthusiast gave the comic another tart. Both deserved an equal portion, the chef said. No pastry need become a casualty of the evening. The comic promised not to misrepresent this as a reward for his jokes. A comparable failure at last year's party had taught him caution. He could be an idiot without advertising it.","Mara ate the raspberry tart the chef had saved. Cassian tried to steal a bite. She lifted the fork away, then offered him half. “Your performance deserves a bonus.”","Cassian was a willing competitor for the last raspberry. He used to do such things at family dinners, his mother had warned her. Mara did not find the habit improved by marriage. 'You can get to the next course without stealing mine,' she told him. He gave her the berry with touching reluctance.","“A titanic one?”","“A sparingly distributed one.”","He smiled, apparently willing to see the reward as sufficient. His attempt at modesty did not last. A moment later he was kissing cream from her finger, with no regard for whether the diners perceived him as respectable. She thought that an excellent use of the terrace's comparative privacy.","Below, the fountain continued its slow music. The recovered ruby rested warm against her skin. She had come for a polished party and ended with rain marks on her shoes, a story she could tell their friends and a husband reluctant to leave her side. She let him remain there until the candles burned low."],"questions":[],"wordCount":762,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0231\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Their guide had promised a gentle trail. Both promises were being tested by a gigantic goat standing on the bridge. The animal ate half the map before Mara could retrieve it. Cassian asked whether the goat would receive a performance bonus for saving them the climb.","The camp offered regional walks for a mixed group of visitors. An Italian painter wanted to sketch the southern ridge. A Japanese linguist and a Mexican mineralogist had come to study quite different things. They agreed to seek out lunch first. Their moderate ambitions seemed compatible with Mara's. The camp marketer, however, advertised every route as productive personal growth.","Their ferry had made its maiden voyage only last spring. A retired major had helped secure its inaugural bunting. His mate, a local practitioner of traditional boatbuilding, knew every plank. The camp was not snobbish about expertise. A master could be found in work clothes as readily as a formal gown, a principle Mara heartily approved.","A pensioner in a battered hat sat beside an elderly mechanic. The pensioner turned out to be a Nobel laureate. He wanted no introduction beyond his name. His nephew, a newcomer to hiking, had already lost a water bottle. A niece with more sense packed another. Their mentor, a local naturalist, divided the load evenly.","A metropolitan minister wore new boots and an uneasy smile. His intimate friend, a philosopher, was delighted by the mud. The rationalist among them wanted to define success before they started. A regional politician replied that success meant everyone returning. By and large, Mara liked that answer. A public figure could be useful without delivering a speech.","The camp marshal was a retired operative who now taught map reading. He checked their names systematically. A school psychiatrist and psychologist had brought colleagues for a rest. They were opposed to turning a day outside into a competitive examination. The guide's tentative suggestion of prizes disappeared. Good judgment would not worsen through a lack of medals.","At the path's first junction stood a weathered statue of Prometheus. A nearby chapel held a faded Madonna. The house's mistress had shown Mara both yesterday, while an old mailman described delivering letters there as a recruit. The family still welcomed regular walkers. Their oldest son, an amateur historian, knew the age of every gate.","A small portrait of a pope hung in the chapel porch. A visiting pensioner had left a name card beside it, asking that the building remain open to any pedestrian who needed shelter. The regulatory notice at the gate agreed. An empty room should not stay vacant simply because people disagreed about prayer. Cassian found that compatible with his own modest ambition to eat.","A juvenile member of the group wanted to be called a pirate. His sister preferred princess. Their grandfather carried a camera and accepted the role of protector. The linguist's niece made a paper monarch butterfly and set it on his hat. A simple game could make outsiders fit in without demanding they explain themselves.","The goat had interrupted that harmony. A militant vegetarian declared it a pioneer of direct action. A marine biologist corrected him: dietary preferences did not explain a goat's desire for paper. The native plants nearby offered plenty of ordinary food. The biologist's specialty was predator and prey relationships at sea, not ruminant diplomacy. Still, he lured it away with grass.","Mara called the goat a manipulator. Cassian called it a monster with excellent taste. A camp marketer tried to photograph the exchange and was asked to wait. Neither Mara nor the goat had consented to perform. The photographer, a probable optimist by temperament, lowered his phone without becoming antagonistic. He found another view.","Mara suffered a small prick from a thorn while freeing the map. Cassian examined her finger. She called him a phony doctor, and he replied that he had never claimed otherwise. His pretty concern spoke volumes about his weakness for her. She enjoyed it more than she intended to show.","A younger guide, the group's most persistent optimist, offered a longer trail. It led past a primitive stone shelter, a defunct quarry and the ruins of a premier resort. A geologist wanted to differentiate the local rocks, and the mineralogist nodded. Their opponent in the route debate, the philosopher, wanted another cup of tea.","An elderly rebel opposed to all schedules asked if a proxy could make his route choice. The marshal said no; each walker should decide. His tentative tone concealed firm sense. Nobody needed an unjustified climb. Mara promised to keep an eye out for the safe turning. In spite of the goat, they would not beg a map to become clearer by staring.","To be sure, nobody had to climb. The marshal asked each respondent to choose. Their choices were modestly divided. Mara chose the ridge. Cassian followed. Worst of all, he was carrying the picnic basket and now suspected his wife's smile had been the goat's reward."],"questions":[],"wordCount":844,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0232\" Cassian kept an eye out for loose stones while Mara followed the regional guide towards a rock shelf. They had chosen to opt for a modest climb, in spite of the camp marketer's attempts to describe it as an expedition. The group's productive morning should not require an unjustified danger. To be sure, the view was incredible enough without exaggeration.","A Japanese linguist and an Italian painter stopped to examine an inscription. The Mexican mineralogist wanted to seek out a distinctive seam in the quarry wall. Their junior guide divided the time evenly. The main guide, a moderate man who disliked being hurried, let them finish. His patience spoke volumes about his experience with enthusiastic visitors.","The pensioner removed his hat in the sun. Mara recognized the Nobel laureate from a photograph, but did not announce it. His nephew wanted to know which stone had the best colour. His niece wanted to know when lunch would happen. The scholar answered both with equal attention. The mechanic helped him adjust a stubborn walking pole.","The camp marketer was checking names systematically, so no pedestrian would accidentally follow the wrong group. Her tentative offer of help met no objection. Even the old rebel opposed to schedules approved of this one. The rule was compatible with his desire to find lunch.","The retired major had been a mentor to Owen when he joined the camp. He carried the group's spare rain covers. A fellow practitioner of map reading checked the junction with him. Their skills let them differentiate two similar paths before the group's optimism could worsen the mistake. Owen's first major lesson had been to ask rather than guess.","The metropolitan minister and his philosopher friend stayed at the rear. Their intimate debate concerned whether a picnic was a philosophical good. A rationalist could measure its calories, the philosopher argued, but that would not explain the pleasure. The public figure who had defined success as returning home agreed. The guide seemed relieved not to be the day's only pragmatist.","The politician remembered his maiden speech with horror. He had asked a colleague to act as his proxy, which was not permitted. His opponent had laughed, then offered advice. A long career could begin with an uneasy minute. The philosopher suggested that this applied to mountains too, and the politician modestly agreed.","At a small spring, the psychiatrist and psychologist filled bottles. A regulatory notice warned against disturbing the stones around the source. They read it and moved on. Nobody needed to become antagonistic about a request that protected the water. A recruit from the camp carried an extra bottle to a thirsty guest, a newcomer who had underestimated the distance.","A walker began to peel an orange. The children asked him to share; he said they could request without having to beg. Cassian used the pause to offer Mara strawberries, which she had not expected him to carry. She called him a manipulator and stole two. The fruit was a pretty good answer to a chilly morning.","The youngest pirate ran ahead, followed by the little princess. Their protector called them back. A monarch butterfly settled on the boy's sleeve, ending the contest. The native plants had attracted several insects. A naturalist explained that a predator's presence did not make every small creature prey at once. The children watched rather than grab. Their first encounter was tentatively peaceful.","From the shelf they could see the chapel below. The faded Madonna was hidden indoors, while Prometheus stood stubbornly outside. The local mailman waved from the lane. He was a regular visitor to the house, whose mistress had put out water for walkers. The marshal reminded the group to return by that gate. Its painted sign was easier to identify than a distant roof.","One child asked why a pope was in the chapel picture while Prometheus stood outside. Her grandfather explained that different people had left different gifts. The gigantic mythic figure was not a native god of this valley, nor proof of a primitive local culture. Mara liked the care he took with that distinction. He was a pioneer in patient answers.","One stone path led towards the old quarry. The guide warned that a juvenile climber might mistake it for an easy route. Primitive walls concealed a deep cut. A militant enthusiast for shortcuts called the concern excessive. The retired operative offered him a chance to inspect it from the proper side. The man declined and did not ask again.","Mara slipped on wet moss and felt Cassian's hand close around hers. Her hip struck a stone lightly. The short fall frightened him more than it hurt her. She promised to stop calling him a phony protector for at least an hour. He kissed her injured finger and asked for a longer guarantee.","Then a shout came from the quarry. The pirate boy's cap had blown across the boundary. A young guide, eager for the camp's performance bonus, had gone after it. A crack split beneath his boot. The huge slab tilted, and he became a prisoner of the gap, one leg wedged in place.","Cassian lowered the picnic basket. data-go=\"231\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"233\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x046c03","title":"A Bonus Paid in Strawberries","volume":"婚后外传：野餐被一座山推迟了","anchor":"chapter-0234","paragraphs":["Mara lay flat on the safe side of the quarry path and spoke to the trapped guide. His name was Owen. He was frightened, angry with himself and still trying to apologize. She asked him to concentrate on her voice. Cassian and the marshal had already moved the other visitors back. Their quiet speed spoke volumes about their concern.","The camp radio worked from the ridge. A rescue team was on its way. The regional coordinator asked the group to remain on the safe route. To be sure, they had useful hands, but they could not see what supported the slab. Trying to shift it blindly might worsen Owen's position. The resulting wait was not an admission of indifference.","The rescue leader checked the route systematically. His calm made Mara feel less like a prisoner of waiting. Their enthusiastic guide, normally an optimist, lay very still. Even now he apologized for failing a respondent's request about the missing cap. She reminded him that the child was safe. He could stop solving that problem.","Mara wanted to seek out a safer view of his trapped boot. The marshal showed her an angle from which she could see without approaching the edge. Owen could move his toes. That encouraged him, though it did not tell them everything about the injury. Mara did not promise what she could not know. She promised to stay.","The rescue team's ropes were laid out systematically. The former operative asked everyone to keep away from them. No snobbish claim to experience would improve the work. Owen might recover quickly, but the probable outcome could not be decided from a safe distance. They had to leave room for the people examining him.","Cassian brought her a groundsheet. The quarry stone had begun to chill her elbows. He tucked one corner around her with the same care he gave a formal coat at a party. She caught his hand briefly. His face was pale beneath the dust. She knew he would have preferred any task that let him exchange places with her.","Worst of all, her elbows had begun to ache, and she did not want Owen to see. A thorn gave her wrist another prick. Cassian slipped a folded coat beneath her arms, the most practical expression of tenderness she could imagine. They would opt for the shorter route. The children did not object. Their grandfather had the recovered cap, which remained remarkably unattractive. Owen laughed when he saw it. Future rewards would concern safe work and teamwork. It was a small policy for a small place, but it mattered. Owen would not lose his job for reporting the mistake. She bit it and discovered that ordinary sweetness could still surprise her.","“You promised me a picnic,” she said.","“I remember. It has been postponed by a mountain.”","He sat beside her. Their shoulders touched. The sunset coloured the clouds above the ridge, and the little chapel's window glowed far below. The camp had grown quiet. Mara leaned her head against him, letting the day's last shiver pass.","He touched his mouth to her temple. “Next time, no goat.”","She laughed. “You cannot promise that.”","“Then I shall promise more
